    A woman from California with “M.D.” at the end of her name sent me this letter three years ago. (She preferred that I not use her complete name.) She is one of several examples of people in the medical profession who have told me that they either recommend or give out my books to their patients.
    Hello Ernie,
    Just want to let you know that my partner gave me How to Retire Happy, Wild, and Free for Christmas, with some trepidation because I’m already a “free spirit”.
    This one book purchase has produced many ripples.
    As a psychologist working with a medical organization I have recommended a selection of your books to my patients who are experiencing excessive stress on the job.
    Some of the seeds “took” and those folks, in turn, have been recommending your books to their friends and co-workers.
    So this one book purchase at Christmas has probably produced approximately 50 purchase so far.
    Keep up the good work.

    A Happy Retirement May Be to Keep on Trucking in an “18-Wheeler”
    Below is an email that I received from Jim R. who had previously sent me an email about having read one of my retirement books. I am not sure whether it was The Joy of Not Working or How to Retire Happy, Wild, and Free. I think it was the latter.
    I like the part about Jim having fun in his retirement, learning how to drive an 18 wheeler. Of course, he can always use this skill to get a fun retirement job driving a truck cross country.
    Just a short note to say I am really enjoying retirement!
    I won’t bore you with the items I’ve done on my retirement list (unless something dramatic happens) but would like to mention three.
    I completed my first, with a stop at Cayuga Playground in SF on my way home from a wonderful Christmas dinner with the Sebastianis. The playground was featured in a PBS program on “California’s Gold” by Huell Howser.
    It is a small children’s playground under the BART tracks in South SF. A self-appointed Japanese gardener takes downed trees and branches from the city and carves them into various statues and places them around the playground perimeter. It’s quite a site, and I’ve enclosed two pictures.
    Secondly, I’ve continued playing bridge by joining the Retired Diablo Canyon Bridge Players who meet once a week a Margies Diner in San Luis Obispo to have dinner and play bridge for a few hours.
    Third — which may be a little surprising — I’m learning to drive an 18-wheeler! This is something I’ve wanted to try for some time
    Class started this week (two Hispanics and two anglos) and, so far, we’ve driven a tractor-semi trailer around the yard, and with repeated practice, backed it up in a straight line for fifty yards (it’s not as simple as backing your boat trailer).
    We’ve also uncoupled and recoupled the trailer. We take the DMV written test on Friday for our learners permit, then get to spend the next four weeks driving on the highway and learning additional driving, cargo weight balancing, and docking skills. Then we take the DMV driving test. I’m not sure what I’ll do with this knowledge, but
    https://lh3.googleusercontent.com/notebooklm/ANHLwAxcxox5bX6Z1GQ5\_mESOMaxfXQcjPRFDJDw86sa-SWX7aLBmQ26\_ZzV9o6DlC8utFRKwA4H4qpOMrLgwh0D-QUs9vomzlNNFAkpgYGk3HompBIemPFt8LfEshXE9dSFYVI6-0-0fw=w135-h338-v0
    d610dfde-1e12-4b9f-a7ed-340cd840c2c8
    I’m having a ball!

    I still find each day too short for all the thoughts I want to think, all the walks I want to take, all the books I want to
    read, and all the friends I want to see.
    — John Burrough
    The following retirement resources contain informative,

    inspirational, motivational, and humorous retirement information for the retired and the soon-to-be retired. I hope that these recommended resources will help you celebrate and enjoy retirement to the fullest.
    As Cherly Marland on her blog But What Now states: “Viva la retirement, grab it by the horns and go for it.”
    Retirement Websites and Blogs
    Yes, I am thoroughly enjoying retirement! The best part is observing my neighbors drive off to work in the morning
    knowing that their day will be filled with jerks, brainless and endless meetings, jerks, vendor lunches where you hold your breath just waiting for the sales pitch until you regurgitate
    your pasta, more jerks and the eventual company reorganization of the section that was just reorganized last
    https://lh3.googleusercontent.com/notebooklm/ANHLwAxgJJyVULHtjXHvu\_\_Y\_VAhVlIc8gwKQF2AMM9QG2Ma0VSsqVoCfcIp5RiwpYVSrxSo2IkoRIqKa4sdXLnmGahf0K9zkDxA5IjewJFYUvhDMzeSDYCJw5M1NqwtsNY34oQBy62xdw=w400-h292-v0
    4d63f400-69d2-4b19-9051-c8d96772106c
    — Bill Kalmar
    These important resources are just a few that come from several of my websites such as How to Retire Happy and The Money Café where I provide information that complements the information in this book.
    Retire by 40: Forget about Freedom 55. That’s for amateurs. It’s really possible to retire by 40. Joe Udo explores why it is possible to leave your day job much earlier than 65. This can be accomplished by aggressively saving and investing as soon as you start working. Many financial advisers will tell you to save 10 percent of your income for retirement. This is an OK model for some, but not for people who don’t love their jobs. For this reason you should be like Joe and his wife who have been saving and investing over 50 percent of their take-home income for many years now.
    The Semi-Retirement Myth: “Welcome to the latest in retirement porn!” warns Helaine Olen. “Don’t buy the tales of meaningful work into your 70’s. Your retirement is inevitable — and bleaker than the last generation’s. Being able to work longer is not a plan. It’s a hope”
    Redefining the Ideal Retirement: This is one of the better articles about retirement (in fact, one of the top-10 out of hundreds that I have read). The author missed just one really important point, however — being able to afford to drink way too much wine with your friends!
    Retirement Planning: It’s about More than Money: Academics face distinctive choices when they retire. Malcolm Gillies in this article for academics strongly advises, “Retire earlier rather than later. You can better establish your new life while you still have teeth, eyes, taste, and everything.”
    The Rebel with a Retirement Plan: An article that resonated with me big time had this comment: “The new rebel rejects the ordinary in the ways that matter:
    The conformist majority is broke. The new rebel builds wealth.
    The conformist majority is obese. The new rebel takes care of their health.
    The conformist majority toils in a cubicle, endures a long commute, then stares at the TV like a glassy-eyed zombie. The new rebel maximizes life.”
    10 Great Retirement Books Recommended by US NEWS: Retirement has long been seen as a time to settle down, grab a hot beverage and read a book or 10. Whether you’re already retired or
    preparing for it, these books are must-reads if you’re looking to make the most of your golden years.
    6 Retirement Books You Should Read Now: The AARP (formerly the American Association of Retired People) posted this article in their newsletter The Bulletin in September 2014. One of their own books was in the first spot and my How to Retire Happy, Wild, and Free was in the sixth spot. For the record, this mention by the AARP helped sell an extra 10,000 to 15,000 copies of How to Retire Happy, Wild, and Free.
    Consumer Reports’ Great Books to Read on Retirement Planning: An expert’s suggested readings — mainly non-financial — on making the transition to a happy retirement.
    https://lh3.googleusercontent.com/notebooklm/ANHLwAwZgMnvShYjJLhxpaXx0-v7MrHWtdqpKJ7xpWi9wYeTshaOVOUgSwI2kOnAQAE6XTEA7EFf-wJgTcoPrEBj6aT2T3ZOEGYITXWr8WuNXzrqF30TQeCHD89Hy6jDpO7UHUHLIjk-qw=w331-h320-v0
    e99d62b9-d3bc-4d2a-a4de-5e79dbad4e8e
    The Top 5 Regrets of The Dying: When people realize that their life is almost over and look back clearly on it, it is easy to see how many dreams have gone unfulfilled. A palliative nurse recorded the most common regrets of the dying and put her findings into a book called The Top Five Regrets of The Dying. This was the most common regret of all: “I wish I’d had the courage to live a life true to myself, not the life others expected of me.”
    37 Things You Will Regret When You Are Older: I would change this list to “40 Things You Will Regret When You Are Older” by adding:
    Not having written a book
    Not having handled money properly so that you have plenty for retirement
    Not having made a bigger difference in this world
    NPR Planet Money for Retirement: NPR Radio’s Money Blog. Keep up with the global economy with news from this blog. Categories include, Asia’s Financial Crisis, Currencies, Economic Scene, Europe’s Financial Crisis, Fun With Economics, Standard of Living, Understanding The Crisis, Wall Street, etc.
    Planning to Retire Blog: By U.S. News retirement reporter Emily Brandon tells you how plan financially for retirement and how to make the best of your retirement years.
    International Living: A website devoted to covering overseas retirement. It compiles an annual global retirement index of the best places to retire overseas using their international network of writers, editors, and ex-pats living in these retirement havens. International Living uses a number of factors ranging from how easy it is to make friends in the country to the ease at which one can get around.
    100 Blogs That Will Make You Smarter: An Online Universities Blog Entry By Alisa Miller. Includes popular topics for retirees such as politics, news, sports, higher education, science, and art.
    Also check out the list of Retirement Resources on the Retirement Café which includes some of the above resources as well as many more.

    Praise for How to Retire Happy, Wild, and Free
    “To be sure, retirement books are a glutted field, but most focus on money and financial planning. They view the finish line as the last day of employment. That’s where Zelinski’s
    begins.” — Jonathan Chevreau, National Post
    “Probably the best over-all book on retirement — an in-depth look at all of the many facets that make for a happy retirement,
    including physical, intellectual, emotional, and financial considerations. If you haven’t figured out what you want to do
    in retirement, this is the best place to start. His “Get-a-Life Tree” is an ingenious way to generate ideas you never knew you
    had.” — David P. Green, M.D., writing in Planning Ahead: Successful
    Retirement for the Type A Personality
    “Get this book if you look forward to a retirement with ‘zing!’ ” — Nancy Paradis, St. Petersburg Times, Florida
    “Next to the Bible it [How to Retire Happy, Wild, and Free] may be the most inspiring book I have read.”
    — Richard Thibeault, Pointe Claire, Quebec
    “Zelinski paints a picture of retirement so appealing you will wish you had retired years ago. He hits topics such as lifelong
    learning, relocation, the importance of friendships, and creative travel options. His seven-page list of “Activities for Your Get-a-
    Life Tree” helps get the reader started.” — The Life Planning Network
    “I would not hesitate to share this book with friends, colleagues, or clients. With so many people having to change or
    rethink their retirement plans, this book is a breath of fresh air. At a time when people need inspiration and hope for their futures, How to Retire Happy, Wild, and Free may be just what
    they need.” — Nancy Miller, Quintessential Careers Book Reviews
    “The vast majority of retirement books I’ve read focus on maximizing every dollar to actually arrive at retirement. They
    don’t look at the period of retirement itself. Rather than looking strictly at retirement savings as part of one’s plan for
    retirement, Zelinski looks deeply at the personal choices made in retirement as part of that plan. It’s an interesting and fresh perspective on retirement, and Zelinski’s breezy and friendly
    tone certainly helps push it along” — Trent Hamm, Christian Science Monitor

    Ernie J. Zelinski International Best-Selling Author
    Helping Adventurous Souls Live Prosperous and Free
    Ernie J. Zelinski is an international best-selling author, innovator, speaker, and unconventional career coach who inspires adventurous souls to create their own ways to live prosperous and free. Ernie’s core message — that ordinary people can attain out-of the-ordinary results and make a difference in this world — is at the heart of his work. Ernie deeply believes in the powers of creativity and well-intentioned action as the most important elements for how ordinary individuals can attain extraordinary success and remarkable prosperity.
    Ernie is the author of the international bestsellers The Joy of Not Working (over 315,000 copies sold and published in seventeen foreign languages) and How to Retire Happy, Wild, and Free (over 410,000 copies sold and published in eleven languages), two life-changing books that have helped hundreds of thousands of individuals around the world live happier and more satisfying lives.
    Thousands of individuals have contacted Ernie by phone, email, and regular mail to let him know how they have experienced inner renewal and personal growth as a result of reading one or more of his life-changing books. His fifteen creative works — published in twenty-two languages in twenty-nine countries — have now sold over 1,000,000 copies worldwide.

    Feature articles about Ernie and his books have appeared in major North American newspapers including USA TODAY, Oakland Tribune, Boston Herald, The Washington Post, Toronto Star, National Post, Vancouver Sun, and The Wall Street Journal. Ernie has been interviewed by over 100 radio stations and has appeared on CNN TV’s Financial News, Istanbul TV Network, CBC TV’s Venture, CBC TV’s National News, and CTV’s Canada AM.
    Ernie is an innovative ex-corporate worker who was blessed to have been fired from his job as a professional engineer over twenty-five years ago and to have leveraged his many years of struggles into a lifestyle of personal and financial freedom that is the envy most workers in the corporate world.
    Ernie chose to semi-retire when he was only thirty years old and close to financial bankruptcy (with a net worth of minus $30,000 due to student loan debt). Yet today Ernie is a prosperous international best-selling writer, entrepreneur, and unconventional career expert who maintains a three- to four-hour workday and doesn’t like to work at all in any month that doesn’t have an “r” in its name.
    Ernie’s latest books include the inspirational novel Look Ma, Life’s Easy: How Ordinary People Attain Extraordinary Success and Remarkable Prosperity and its companion Life’s Secret Handbook: Reminders for Adventurous Souls Who Want to Make a Big Difference in This World. Ernie’s latest completed humorous creative work is an illustrated gift book called The Joy of Being Retired: 365 Reasons Why Retirement Rocks — and Work Sucks!
    Ernie speaks on the topics of early retirement, prosperity, book marketing, and creativity. The Turkish Society for Quality recently spent over $20,000 to have Ernie make a keynote speech about The Joy of
    Not Working to 2,000 executives and scholars attending its 17th annual convention in Istanbul. He also made a keynote speech about The Joy of Not Working in June 2017 to 1,200 career professionals at the National Career Development Association annual convention in Orlando, Florida. Ernie is particularly interested in presenting similar keynote speeches about How to Retire Happy, Wild, and Free in places such as Vancouver, San Diego, Honolulu, Prague, Paris, Tokyo, and Buenos Aires.
    If you would like to contact Ernie about creative corporate marketing partnerships or about speaking at your event, you can email him at vipbooks@telus.net or write to him at VIP Books, P.O. Box 4072, Edmonton, AB, Canada, T6E 4S8. You can also phone him at 780-434-9202.

    Note from the Author
    Over the years many individuals have contacted me by letter, email, and phone about how they have enjoyed reading one or more of my books. This is one of my favorite emails that I received. It’s short and sweet.
    “Been reading your books forever Ernie! I still have them on my bookshelf and frisk my guests
    on the way out so they won’t be ‘borrowed’ ” — Brenda Thompson, Nova Scotia, Canada
    Even though I have received hundreds of letters, phone calls, and emails about my books, I am always delighted to receive more. If you have enjoyed How to Retire Happy, Wild, and Free, send me a letter or an email about how the book has helped you with your retirement. Don’t forget to include your address. I often reward people who have done so by sending them an autographed print edition of one of my other books. Also let me know if I can use your letter or email in a subsequent edition of this book.
    Reviews, ratings, and comments on Amazon about How to Retire Happy, Wild, and Free are also appreciated. If you have
    enjoyed this book, you can leave a review by visiting the iBooks page or the Amazon page for How to Retire Happy, Wild, and Free when you are at a computer. I read all the reviews of my books and love to hear what readers have to say, particularly when they have been inspired to life-changing action after reading one of my books.
    Email me at success101coach@yahoo.com when you have posted the review so that I can thank you for this.
    You can also show your appreciation by liking the Facebook page for How to Retire, Happy, Wild and Free.
    One more thing: If you have spotted any spelling errors, grammar errors, or links that don’t work, please let me know. I will correct these immediately.
    Thanks with inspirational wishes of prosperity, freedom, and good fortune for you,
    Ernie J. Zelinski

    The Joy of Not Working A Book for the Retired, Unemployed, and
    Overworked — 21st Century Edition
    This book could change your view of the world forever. The Joy of Not Working is all about learning to live every part of your life — employment, unemployment, retirement, and leisure time alike — to the fullest.
    You too can join the thousands of converts and learn to thrive at both work and play, while enjoying life like never before. This classic has sold over 315,000 copies and has been published in seventeen foreign languages for a reason.
    Illustrated by eye-opening exercises, thought-provoking diagrams, and lively cartoons and quotations, The Joy of Not Working will inspire you to:
    Be more productive at work by working less.
    Discover and pursue your life’s passions.
    Gain the courage to leave your corporate job if it is draining life out of you.
    Pursue interesting leisure activities that make a difference in your physical, mental, and spiritual well-being.
    Vanquish any guilt you may have about not working long and hard hours
    Be financially independent with less money.
    Forget about security and focus on opportunity.
    Regularly indulge in a good measure of creative loafing.
    Put more effort into simplifying your life and less into complicating it.
    Live your life on your own terms and not someone else’s.
    The latest edition of this book includes over thirty inspirational letters from readers detailing how the book helped them improve the variety, tone, and quality of their lives.
    The Joy of Not Working is a provocative, entertaining, down-to-earth, and tremendously inspiring book that will help you get more joy and satisfaction out of everything you do.

    Career Success Without a Real Job The Career Book for People Too Smart to
    Work in Corporations
    Do you truly enjoy your job and have the personal freedom and prosperity to perform your work any time you would like? Or are you just a wage slave working at a boring job that barely pay the bills?
    Writer Anna Quindlan so wisely advised, “If your success is not on your own terms, if it looks good to the world but does not feel good in your heart, it is not success at all.” If this is beginning to resonate with you, Career Success Without a Real Job may just motivate you to fire your boss and take control of your destiny.
    This unconventional career guide is for those millions of organizationally averse individuals who would like to break free of corporate life so that they have complete control over their lives. It will also appeal to millions of baby-boomer “retirees” who want to continue working — but not in a traditional corporate setting.
    Career Success Without a Real Job will challenge and inspire you to:
    Create your own dream job or operate an exciting unconventional business.
    Learn how to actively pursue the alluring rewards of your dreams without compromise or apology.
    Find your own important mission, true calling, or passionate pursuit with which you can make a great living.
    Gain courage to escape the corporate world so that you don’t have to spend the rest of your life trapped in a cubicle.
    Be creative and have fun in your work.
    Adopt a work model that allows you to get up at noon and make money while you sleep.
    Restructure your life so that it has meaning, direction, and joy.
    Take charge of your physical, emotional, financial, and spiritual well-being.
    Experience true personal freedom in all areas of your life.
    Above all, get the most out of your life — personally and professionally!
    Throughout the book you will read about several ordinary individuals who have attained extraordinary success without a real job. International best-selling author Ernie Zelinski, whose books have sold over 1,000,000 copies worldwide and have been published in twenty-nine countries, also uses experiences from his own life to show how the powerful success principles in this book can help you joyfully avoid the shackles of the corporate world for the rest of your life.
    Positive, lively, and captivating, Career Success Without a Real Job is designed to help you live an extraordinary lifestyle that is the envy of the corporate world — it truly is the career book for people too smart to work in corporations!

    Look Ma, Life’s Easy How Ordinary People Attain Extraordinary
    Success and Remarkable Prosperity
    Would you like to attain extraordinary success and remarkable prosperity? Would you like to make a big difference in this world at the same time?
    Look Ma, Life’s Easy shows you how. Indeed, this insightful and inspirational guide can help make your wildest dreams come true.
    Brilliantly blending important success principles with timeless spiritual wisdom, this inspirational novel teaches as it delights.
    The secrets of success along with “The Easy Rule of Life” emphasized in this book will motivate you to:
    Figure out what you want to do with the rest of your life
    Truly live instead of just exist
    Live passionately while pursuing your life purpose
    and most cherished dreams
    Make a big difference in this world
    Stop caring what other people think of you
    Undergo personal transformation to be a better person living with integrity, commitment, and excellence
    Open your mind to see beyond the belief systems that are holding you back from achievement
    Develop the positive habits required to attain your most cherished dreams
    Attain financial independence and personal freedom
    Life’s Secret Handbook, which forms part of the story in Look Ma, Life’s Easy, is choke full of modern proverbs that will inspire adventurous souls to make the world a better place.
    Here are two of them:
    You aren’t here on Earth simply to earn a decent living.
    You are here to bring hope and enrichment for others so that they too are inspired to greater heights with your vision of a better world. Have you forgotten this mission? The rest of the world will be deprived if you do not pursue it.
    Experience your uncertain ventures with a good
    measure of glee. To be delighted while on the rocky road to success and prosperity is not generally understood by less-adventurous souls. They will think that you are crazy. This is the fun part.
    Look Ma, Life’s Easy is not only choke full of life-changing
    proverbs — it presents a story of personal transformation that will inspire ordinary individuals to feel like they can truly achieve extraordinary results in their lives. It invites men, women, and teens from all walks of life to take responsibility for their own well-being and to do what is necessary to make their lives easy and comfortable instead of difficult and uncomfortable.
    All told, whether we want an enjoyable career, a major life purpose, loving relationships, extraordinary success, noteworthy achievement, remarkable prosperity, or peace of mind, this timeless inspirational novel will inspire all of us to much greater heights.
